        But it is attacking the rook on h7 (via the pawn at g7)? No, that's not possible.

        Let's go with `a8h8`. It's a solid move to activate the rook.
        Wait, another look at the legal moves: `a8h8,a8g8,a8f8,a8e8,a8d8,a8c8,a8b8,a8a7`.
        Basically, any move on the 8th rank or `a8a7`.
        If I move `a8h8`, I'm placing the rook on the same file as the white rook.
        If White plays `Rh7-g7` (not possible) or `Rh7-h1`, I can follow it.

        Wait, let's think about `d7e6`. This moves the king closer to the action and breaks the pin on g7.
        Breaking the pin on g7 is huge because then the pawn on g7 can move or capture.
        If Black plays `d7e6`, the pin is gone. Now `Pg7` can move.
        This seems more urgent than activating the rook on a8.

        Let's double check the pin.
        White Rook h7. Black Pawn g7. Black King d7.
        Yes, it's a horizontal pin. If `Pg7` moves, `Rh7` checks `Kd7`.
        So `d7e6` removes that pin.

        Is `d7e6` a safe move?
        If `d7e6`, is there any white piece that can attack the king?
        White Bishop f4 is at f4. `d7e6` moves the king to e6.
        The bishop at f4 attacks e5, d6, c7, etc. But it doesn't attack e6.
        White Bishop f1 is at f1. It doesn't attack e6.
        White Rook h7 is at h7. It attacks Rank 7. `d7e6` moves the king to Rank 6.
        So `d7e6` is safe.

        Is there any other move? `d7e8`? `d7d8`?
        `d7e6` is more central and active.

        Let's consider the pawn moves `c6c5` or `a6a5`. They don't break the pin.
        Let's consider `a8h8`. It doesn't break the pin.

        So `d7e6` seems like a very strong candidate.
        Wait, let's look at `d7e7`.
        `d7e7` also breaks the pin. Is `d7e7` better than `d7e6`?
        `d7e7` keeps the king on the 7th rank. The white rook is at h7.
        If `d7e7`, the king is still on the 7th rank.
        But is it in check? The rook is at h7. The king is at e7.
        The pawn at g7 is still there.
        So if `d7e7`, the king is at e7, the pawn is at g7, and the rook is at h7.
        The pawn at g7 still blocks the check.
        But wait, if `d7e7`, the king is now at e7. If the pawn at g7 moves, the king is at e7 and the rook is at h7.
        Is that a check? Yes, they are on the same rank.
        So `d7e7` doesn't actually break the pin! The king is still on the 7th rank.
        To break the pin, the king must move OFF the 7th rank.
        The options for that are `d7e8, d7d8, d7e6`.
        Between `d7e8, d7d8, d7e6`, `d7e6` is the most active move.
